## QAB-348 · L1 · easy · Pie chart interpretation · foundation

A pie chart shows how a family's monthly income of ₹60,000 is distributed. The sector shares are:

| Head | Share of income |
|---|---|
| Rent | 25% |
| Food | 30% |
| Education | 15% |
| Transport | 10% |
| Savings | 12% |
| Others | 8% |

By how much does the monthly Rent exceed the Transport expenditure?

- **A.** ₹15,000  _(error: gave the Rent itself)_
- **B.** ₹7,800  _(error: subtracted Savings instead of Transport)_
- **C.** ₹10,200  _(error: subtracted Others instead of Transport)_
- **D.** ₹9,000 ✅

**Working**

1. Difference in share = 25% − 10% = 15%
2. 15% of 60,000 = 9,000

**Formula:** Difference = (share₁ − share₂) × total  
**Trap:** Subtract shares first.

A pie chart shows how a family's monthly income of ₹60,000 is distributed. The sector shares are:

## QAB-351 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

A company has 3,600 employees. Pie chart 1 gives their distribution across departments; the table gives the percentage of women in each department.

| Department | Share of employees | Women in department |
|---|---|---|
| HR | 10% | 60% |
| Sales | 25% | 30% |
| Operations | 20% | 25% |
| IT | 22.5% | 40% |
| Finance | 12.5% | 48% |
| Admin | 10% | 50% |

How many women work in the company?

- **A.** 2,214  _(error: counted men instead)_
- **B.** 1,206  _(error: left out Admin)_
- **C.** 1,386 ✅
- **D.** 1,518  _(error: averaged the six women percentages)_

**Working**

1. Women: HR 216, Sales 270, Ops 180, IT 324, Finance 216, Admin 180
2. Total = 1,386

**Formula:** Sum of department-wise values  
**Trap:** Department sizes differ, so percentages cannot be averaged.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-352 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

A company has 3,600 employees. Pie chart 1 gives their distribution across departments; the table gives the percentage of women in each department.

| Department | Share of employees | Women in department |
|---|---|---|
| HR | 10% | 60% |
| Sales | 25% | 30% |
| Operations | 20% | 25% |
| IT | 22.5% | 40% |
| Finance | 12.5% | 48% |
| Admin | 10% | 50% |

What is the ratio of men in Sales to men in IT?

- **A.** 20 : 21  _(error: swapped the women percentages of the two departments)_
- **B.** 35 : 27 ✅
- **C.** 5 : 6  _(error: compared women)_
- **D.** 10 : 9  _(error: compared all employees)_

**Working**

1. Sales men = 900 × 70% = 630
2. IT men = 810 × 60% = 486
3. 630 : 486 = 35 : 27

**Formula:** Men = employees × (100 − women %)  
**Trap:** Use each department's own percentage.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-353 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

A company has 3,600 employees. Pie chart 1 gives their distribution across departments; the table gives the percentage of women in each department.

| Department | Share of employees | Women in department |
|---|---|---|
| HR | 10% | 60% |

| Finance | 12.5% | 48% |
| Admin | 10% | 50% |

- **B.** 12%  _(error: divided by all employees)_
- **C.** 31.17% ✅
- **D.** 22.5%  _(error: gave the departments' share of employees)_

1. Finance + HR women = 216 + 216 = 432
2. 432/1,386 × 100 = 31.17%

**Formula:** Share = part / whole × 100  
**Trap:** The whole here is all women.

## QAB-354 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

A company has 3,600 employees. Pie chart 1 gives their distribution across departments; the table gives the percentage of women in each department.

| Department | Share of employees | Women in department |
|---|---|---|
| HR | 10% | 60% |
| Sales | 25% | 30% |
| Operations | 20% | 25% |
| IT | 22.5% | 40% |
| Finance | 12.5% | 48% |
| Admin | 10% | 50% |

If 20% of the men in Operations are transferred to Admin, what is the new ratio of men to women in Admin?

- **A.** 9 : 5  _(error: transferred 20% of all Operations staff)_
- **B.** 8 : 5 ✅
- **C.** 5 : 8  _(error: reversed the ratio)_
- **D.** 1 : 1  _(error: ignored the transfer)_

**Working**

1. Operations men = 720 × 75% = 540; 20% = 108
2. Admin men = 180 + 108 = 288; women = 180
3. 288 : 180 = 8 : 5

**Formula:** Update the count, then form the ratio  
**Trap:** Only men move; the base is Operations men.

## QAB-355 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

A company has 3,600 employees. Pie chart 1 gives their distribution across departments; the table gives the percentage of women in each department.

| Department | Share of employees | Women in department |
|---|---|---|
| HR | 10% | 60% |
| Sales | 25% | 30% |
| Operations | 20% | 25% |
| IT | 22.5% | 40% |
| Finance | 12.5% | 48% |
| Admin | 10% | 50% |

What is the central angle of the department that has the largest number of women?

- **A.** 90°  _(error: chose the largest department)_
- **B.** 144°  _(error: used IT's women percentage as the share)_
- **C.** 81° ✅
- **D.** 36°  _(error: chose HR, which has the highest women percentage)_

**Working**

1. Largest number of women: IT (324)
2. IT share = 22.5% ⇒ 22.5 × 3.6 = 81°

**Formula:** Angle = share × 360°  
**Trap:** Highest % is not highest number.

## QAB-356 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

Two pie charts show a company's expenditure distribution. Total expenditure was ₹40 crore in 2022 and ₹50 crore in 2023.

| Head | 2022 share | 2023 share |
|---|---|---|
| Raw material | 35% | 32% |
| Salaries | 20% | 22% |
| Marketing | 12% | 14% |
| R&D | 8% | 10% |
| Transport | 10% | 9% |
| Others | 15% | 13% |

By what percentage did expenditure on Salaries increase from 2022 to 2023?

- **A.** 25%  _(error: used the growth of total expenditure)_
- **B.** 27.27%  _(error: used 2023 as base)_
- **C.** 10%  _(error: compared the shares only)_
- **D.** 37.5% ✅

**Working**

1. 2022: 20% of 40 = ₹8 crore; 2023: 22% of 50 = ₹11 crore
2. (11 − 8)/8 × 100 = 37.5%

**Formula:** Convert shares to amounts before comparing  
**Trap:** Totals differ between the two pies.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-357 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

Two pie charts show a company's expenditure distribution. Total expenditure was ₹40 crore in 2022 and ₹50 crore in 2023.

| Head | 2022 share | 2023 share |
|---|---|---|
| Raw material | 35% | 32% |
| Salaries | 20% | 22% |
| Marketing | 12% | 14% |
| R&D | 8% | 10% |
| Transport | 10% | 9% |
| Others | 15% | 13% |

For how many heads was the rupee expenditure in 2023 LOWER than in 2022?

- **A.** 1  _(error: counted only Raw material)_
- **B.** 2  _(error: counted Transport and Others)_
- **C.** 3  _(error: counted heads whose share fell)_
- **D.** 0 ✅

**Working**

1. 2023 amounts: 16, 11, 7, 5, 4.5, 6.5 vs 2022: 14, 8, 4.8, 3.2, 4, 6
2. Every head rose in rupees ⇒ 0

**Formula:** Compare amounts, not shares  
**Trap:** A smaller share of a larger total can still be more money.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

| Transport | 10% | 9% |
| Others | 15% | 13% |

By how much did combined Marketing and R&D expenditure rise from 2022 to 2023?

- **B.** ₹2 crore  _(error: applied the change in share to the 2023 total)_

**Working**

1. 2023: 24% of 50 = 12
2. 2022: 20% of 40 = 8
3. Rise = ₹4 crore

**Formula:** Change = amount₂ − amount₁

Two pie charts show a company's expenditure distribution. Total expenditure was ₹40 crore in 2022 and ₹50 crore in 2023.

What is the central angle of Transport in the 2023 pie chart?

- **B.** 32.4° ✅
- **C.** 9°  _(error: used the percentage as degrees)_

## QAB-360 · L4 · hard · Pie chart interpretation · officer

Two pie charts show a company's expenditure distribution. Total expenditure was ₹40 crore in 2022 and ₹50 crore in 2023.

| Head | 2022 share | 2023 share |
|---|---|---|
| Raw material | 35% | 32% |
| Salaries | 20% | 22% |
| Marketing | 12% | 14% |
| R&D | 8% | 10% |
| Transport | 10% | 9% |
| Others | 15% | 13% |

In 2024 total expenditure rises 10% over 2023 and the R&D share rises to 12%. What is the R&D expenditure in 2024?

- **A.** ₹6.6 crore ✅
- **B.** ₹5.6 crore  _(error: raised the 2023 R&D amount by 12%)_
- **C.** ₹5.5 crore  _(error: kept the R&D share at 10%)_
- **D.** ₹6 crore  _(error: did not raise the total)_

**Working**

1. 2024 total = 55
2. 12% of 55 = ₹6.6 crore

**Formula:** Amount = new share × new total  
**Trap:** Both the total and the share change.

## QAB-361 · L1 · easy · Caselet data interpretation · foundation

A school has 1,200 students, of whom 55% are boys. 40% of the boys and 60% of the girls take part in sports; the rest do not take part.

How many girls take part in sports?

- **A.** 216  _(error: used 40% for girls)_
- **B.** 360  _(error: assumed half the students are girls)_
- **C.** 264  _(error: computed boys)_
- **D.** 324 ✅

**Working**

1. Girls = 45% of 1,200 = 540
2. 60% of 540 = 324

**Formula:** Part = % × base  
**Trap:** Girls are 45%, not 50%.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-362 · L1 · easy · Caselet data interpretation · foundation

A school has 1,200 students, of whom 55% are boys. 40% of the boys and 60% of the girls take part in sports; the rest do not take part.

How many students do not take part in sports?

- **A.** 612 ✅
- **B.** 588  _(error: gave the participants)_
- **C.** 396  _(error: counted non-participating boys only)_
- **D.** 600  _(error: averaged 40% and 60%)_

**Working**

1. Participants = 264 + 324 = 588
2. Non-participants = 1,200 − 588 = 612

**Formula:** Complement = total − part  
**Trap:** Percentages apply to different bases.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-363 · L1 · easy · Caselet data interpretation · foundation

A school has 1,200 students, of whom 55% are boys. 40% of the boys and 60% of the girls take part in sports; the rest do not take part.

What percentage of the sports participants are boys?

- **A.** 55%  _(error: gave the share of boys in the school)_
- **B.** 44.9% ✅
- **C.** 40%  _(error: gave the participation rate of boys)_
- **D.** 22%  _(error: divided by all students)_

**Working**

1. Boy participants = 264, total participants = 588
2. 264/588 × 100 = 44.90%

**Formula:** Share = part / whole × 100  
**Trap:** The whole is participants.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-364 · L1 · easy · Caselet data interpretation · foundation

A school has 1,200 students, of whom 55% are boys. 40% of the boys and 60% of the girls take part in sports; the rest do not take part.

What is the ratio of boys who do not take part to girls who do not take part?

- **A.** 22 : 27  _(error: compared participants)_
- **B.** 6 : 11  _(error: reversed the ratio)_
- **C.** 11 : 6 ✅
- **D.** 11 : 9  _(error: compared all boys and girls)_

**Working**

1. Boys not taking part = 396
2. Girls not taking part = 216
3. 396 : 216 = 11 : 6

**Formula:** Ratio of complements  
**Trap:** Use the non-participating groups.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-365 · L1 · easy · Caselet data interpretation · foundation

A school has 1,200 students, of whom 55% are boys. 40% of the boys and 60% of the girls take part in sports; the rest do not take part.

If 30 more girls join sports, what percentage of girls will take part?

- **A.** 62.11%  _(error: added 30 to the number of girls as well)_
- **B.** 65.56% ✅
- **C.** 62.5%  _(error: divided 30 by all students and added to 60%)_
- **D.** 29.5%  _(error: divided by all students)_

**Working**

1. New participants = 354
2. 354/540 × 100 = 65.56%

**Formula:** Share = part / base × 100  
**Trap:** The number of girls is unchanged.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-366 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A company sold 12,000 units of three products X, Y and Z in two cities, Delhi and Pune. Delhi accounted for 60% of the units. In Delhi, X, Y and Z were sold in the ratio 5 : 4 : 3. In Pune, Y made up 40% of the city's units, and 600 more units of X than of Z were sold. Selling prices per unit are ₹250 for X, ₹300 for Y and ₹400 for Z in both cities.

How many units of X were sold in the two cities together?

- **A.** 4,140  _(error: reversed 'X exceeds Z by 600' in Pune)_
- **B.** 4,740 ✅
- **C.** 4,920  _(error: took Pune's Y figure)_
- **D.** 4,440  _(error: split Pune's X + Z equally)_

**Working**

1. Delhi: 7,200 in 5:4:3 ⇒ X 3,000
2. Pune: 4,800; Y = 1,920; X + Z = 2,880; X − Z = 600 ⇒ X = 1,740
3. Total X = 4,740

**Formula:** Sum and difference ⇒ X = (S + D)/2  
**Trap:** Read which product is larger.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-367 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A company sold 12,000 units of three products X, Y and Z in two cities, Delhi and Pune. Delhi accounted for 60% of the units. In Delhi, X, Y and Z were sold in the ratio 5 : 4 : 3. In Pune, Y made up 40% of the city's units, and 600 more units of X than of Z were sold. Selling prices per unit are ₹250 for X, ₹300 for Y and ₹400 for Z in both cities.

Units of Z sold in Pune are what percentage of units of Z sold in Delhi?

- **A.** 96.67%  _(error: used Pune's X)_
- **B.** 63.33% ✅
- **C.** 80%  _(error: split Pune's X + Z equally)_
- **D.** 157.89%  _(error: inverted the ratio)_

**Working**

1. Pune Z = (2,880 − 600)/2 = 1,140
2. 1,140/1,800 × 100 = 63.33%

**Formula:** A as % of B = A/B × 100  
**Trap:** Solve Pune's X and Z first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-368 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A company sold 12,000 units of three products X, Y and Z in two cities, Delhi and Pune. Delhi accounted for 60% of the units. In Delhi, X, Y and Z were sold in the ratio 5 : 4 : 3. In Pune, Y made up 40% of the city's units, and 600 more units of X than of Z were sold. Selling prices per unit are ₹250 for X, ₹300 for Y and ₹400 for Z in both cities.

What is the ratio of the total units of Y to the total units of Z?

- **A.** 72 : 59  _(error: swapped X and Z in Pune)_
- **B.** 72 : 49 ✅
- **C.** 49 : 72  _(error: reversed the ratio)_
- **D.** 4 : 3  _(error: used Delhi's ratio only)_

**Working**

1. Y = 2,400 + 1,920 = 4,320
2. Z = 1,800 + 1,140 = 2,940
3. 4,320 : 2,940 = 72 : 49

**Formula:** Ratio of totals  
**Trap:** Combine both cities.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-369 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A company sold 12,000 units of three products X, Y and Z in two cities, Delhi and Pune. Delhi accounted for 60% of the units. In Delhi, X, Y and Z were sold in the ratio 5 : 4 : 3. In Pune, Y made up 40% of the city's units, and 600 more units of X than of Z were sold. Selling prices per unit are ₹250 for X, ₹300 for Y and ₹400 for Z in both cities.

What was the total revenue from sales in Pune?

- **A.** ₹15.57 lakh  _(error: swapped X and Z in Pune)_
- **B.** ₹15.12 lakh  _(error: split X + Z equally)_
- **C.** ₹14.67 lakh ✅
- **D.** ₹21.90 lakh  _(error: computed Delhi's revenue)_

**Working**

1. X: 1,740 × 250 = 4,35,000
2. Y: 1,920 × 300 = 5,76,000
3. Z: 1,140 × 400 = 4,56,000
4. Total = ₹14.67 lakh

**Formula:** Revenue = Σ units × price  
**Trap:** Keep X and Z straight; Z is dearer.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-370 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A company sold 12,000 units of three products X, Y and Z in two cities, Delhi and Pune. Delhi accounted for 60% of the units. In Delhi, X, Y and Z were sold in the ratio 5 : 4 : 3. In Pune, Y made up 40% of the city's units, and 600 more units of X than of Z were sold. Selling prices per unit are ₹250 for X, ₹300 for Y and ₹400 for Z in both cities.

Units of Y sold in Delhi are what percentage more than units of Y sold in Pune?

- **A.** 10%  _(error: divided by Pune's total units)_
- **B.** 25% ✅
- **C.** 16.67%  _(error: compared Y's shares of each city's sales)_
- **D.** 20%  _(error: used Delhi as base)_

**Working**

1. Delhi Y = 2,400; Pune Y = 1,920
2. 480/1,920 × 100 = 25%

**Formula:** % more = difference / smaller × 100  
**Trap:** 'More than Pune' ⇒ Pune is the base.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-371 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A bank branch gave loans to 800 customers in a year under three schemes: home, vehicle and personal. Home-loan customers were 35% of the total, and vehicle-loan customers were 80 more than personal-loan customers. The average loan was ₹24 lakh for home, ₹6 lakh for vehicle and ₹2.5 lakh for personal loans. Women made up 15% of home-loan, 25% of vehicle-loan and 40% of personal-loan customers.

What was the total amount disbursed?

- **A.** ₹86.67 crore  _(error: multiplied 800 by the simple average of the three averages)_
- **B.** ₹87.9 crore  _(error: swapped vehicle and personal customers)_
- **C.** ₹89.3 crore  _(error: split the remaining customers equally)_
- **D.** ₹90.7 crore ✅

**Working**

1. Home 280, vehicle 300, personal 220
2. 280 × 24 + 300 × 6 + 220 × 2.5 = 6,720 + 1,800 + 550 = 9,070 lakh
3. = ₹90.70 crore

**Formula:** Total = Σ count × average  
**Trap:** Averages must be weighted by counts.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-372 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A bank branch gave loans to 800 customers in a year under three schemes: home, vehicle and personal. Home-loan customers were 35% of the total, and vehicle-loan customers were 80 more than personal-loan customers. The average loan was ₹24 lakh for home, ₹6 lakh for vehicle and ₹2.5 lakh for personal loans. Women made up 15% of home-loan, 25% of vehicle-loan and 40% of personal-loan customers.

How many women took loans?

- **A.** 217  _(error: swapped vehicle and personal customers)_
- **B.** 595  _(error: counted men)_
- **C.** 205 ✅
- **D.** 211  _(error: split the remaining customers equally)_

**Working**

1. Home: 15% of 280 = 42
2. Vehicle: 25% of 300 = 75
3. Personal: 40% of 220 = 88
4. Total = 205

**Formula:** Σ count × rate  
**Trap:** Solve vehicle and personal counts first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-373 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A bank branch gave loans to 800 customers in a year under three schemes: home, vehicle and personal. Home-loan customers were 35% of the total, and vehicle-loan customers were 80 more than personal-loan customers. The average loan was ₹24 lakh for home, ₹6 lakh for vehicle and ₹2.5 lakh for personal loans. Women made up 15% of home-loan, 25% of vehicle-loan and 40% of personal-loan customers.

Personal loans form what percentage of the total amount disbursed?

- **A.** 27.5%  _(error: gave the share of customers)_
- **B.** 6.06% ✅
- **C.** 7.69%  _(error: compared average loan sizes)_
- **D.** 6.46%  _(error: left personal loans out of the base)_

**Working**

1. Personal = 220 × 2.5 = 550 lakh
2. 550/9,070 × 100 = 6.06%

**Formula:** Share = part / total × 100  
**Trap:** Share of money ≠ share of customers.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-374 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A bank branch gave loans to 800 customers in a year under three schemes: home, vehicle and personal. Home-loan customers were 35% of the total, and vehicle-loan customers were 80 more than personal-loan customers. The average loan was ₹24 lakh for home, ₹6 lakh for vehicle and ₹2.5 lakh for personal loans. Women made up 15% of home-loan, 25% of vehicle-loan and 40% of personal-loan customers.

If the average vehicle loan rises to ₹7 lakh and the number of vehicle-loan customers falls by 10%, by what percentage does the vehicle-loan disbursement change?

- **A.** 6.67%  _(error: subtracted the two percentage changes)_
- **B.** 16.67%  _(error: ignored the fall in customers)_
- **C.** 5% ✅
- **D.** 4.76%  _(error: used the new amount as base)_

**Working**

1. New = 270 × 7 = 1,890 lakh; old = 1,800 lakh
2. Change = 90/1,800 × 100 = +5%

**Formula:** (1 + a)(1 + b) − 1  
**Trap:** Percentage changes multiply, not add.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-375 · L4 · hard · Caselet data interpretation · officer

A bank branch gave loans to 800 customers in a year under three schemes: home, vehicle and personal. Home-loan customers were 35% of the total, and vehicle-loan customers were 80 more than personal-loan customers. The average loan was ₹24 lakh for home, ₹6 lakh for vehicle and ₹2.5 lakh for personal loans. Women made up 15% of home-loan, 25% of vehicle-loan and 40% of personal-loan customers.

What is the ratio of women home-loan customers to women personal-loan customers?

- **A.** 21 : 44 ✅
- **B.** 44 : 21  _(error: reversed the ratio)_
- **C.** 14 : 11  _(error: compared all customers)_
- **D.** 3 : 8  _(error: compared the percentages)_

**Working**

1. Women home = 42; women personal = 88
2. 42 : 88 = 21 : 44

**Formula:** Ratio of derived counts  
**Trap:** Apply each rate to its own base.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-376 · L1 · easy · Set-based data interpretation · foundation

In a survey of 500 people, 280 read newspaper A, 240 read newspaper B and 90 read both.

How many people read only newspaper A?

- **A.** 150  _(error: gave the readers of only B)_
- **B.** 90  _(error: gave the readers of both)_
- **C.** 280  _(error: did not remove the readers of both)_
- **D.** 190 ✅

**Working**

1. Only A = 280 − 90 = 190

**Formula:** Only A = n(A) − n(A ∩ B)  
**Trap:** Readers of both are inside A.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-377 · L1 · easy · Set-based data interpretation · foundation

In a survey of 500 people, 280 read newspaper A, 240 read newspaper B and 90 read both.

How many people read neither newspaper?

- **A.** 220  _(error: subtracted only the readers of A)_
- **B.** 90  _(error: gave the readers of both)_
- **C.** 160  _(error: left out the readers of both)_
- **D.** 70 ✅

**Working**

1. n(A ∪ B) = 280 + 240 − 90 = 430
2. Neither = 500 − 430 = 70

**Formula:** n(A ∪ B) = n(A) + n(B) − n(A ∩ B)  
**Trap:** Subtract the overlap once.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-378 · L1 · easy · Set-based data interpretation · foundation

In a survey of 500 people, 280 read newspaper A, 240 read newspaper B and 90 read both.

How many people read exactly one newspaper?

- **A.** 430  _(error: included the readers of both)_
- **B.** 520  _(error: added the totals of A and B)_
- **C.** 340 ✅
- **D.** 280  _(error: gave the readers of A)_

**Working**

1. Only A 190 + only B 150 = 340

**Formula:** Exactly one = n(A) + n(B) − 2n(A ∩ B)  
**Trap:** Remove the overlap from both sets.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-379 · L1 · easy · Set-based data interpretation · foundation

In a survey of 500 people, 280 read newspaper A, 240 read newspaper B and 90 read both.

What percentage of the readers of B also read A?

- **A.** 37.5% ✅
- **B.** 32.14%  _(error: used readers of A as base)_
- **C.** 62.5%  _(error: gave the share reading only B)_
- **D.** 18%  _(error: used all surveyed people)_

**Working**

1. 90/240 × 100 = 37.5%

**Formula:** Conditional share = n(A ∩ B)/n(B) × 100  
**Trap:** The base is readers of B.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-380 · L1 · easy · Set-based data interpretation · foundation

In a survey of 500 people, 280 read newspaper A, 240 read newspaper B and 90 read both.

What is the ratio of people who read only A to those who read only B?

- **A.** 19 : 15 ✅
- **B.** 15 : 19  _(error: reversed the ratio)_
- **C.** 7 : 6  _(error: used total readers)_
- **D.** 19 : 9  _(error: compared only A with both)_

**Working**

1. 190 : 150 = 19 : 15

**Formula:** Ratio of regions  
**Trap:** Use the 'only' regions.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-381 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

A survey of 1,000 people asked which of three apps P, Q and R they use. Results: 370 use P, 360 use Q and 320 use R. 100 use both P and Q, 120 use both Q and R, 190 use exactly two of the apps and 40 use all three.

How many people use only P?

- **A.** 220 ✅
- **B.** 230  _(error: subtracted all-three again instead of the P-and-R-only region)_
- **C.** 180  _(error: subtracted P∩Q and P∩R without adding back the all-three group)_
- **D.** 270  _(error: forgot the P-and-R-only region)_

**Working**

1. P and Q only = 100 − 40 = 60; Q and R only = 120 − 40 = 80
2. P and R only = 190 − 60 − 80 = 50
3. Only P = 370 − 60 − 50 − 40 = 220

**Formula:** Region method for three sets  
**Trap:** Separate 'both' totals into 'exactly two' and 'all three'.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-382 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

A survey of 1,000 people asked which of three apps P, Q and R they use. Results: 370 use P, 360 use Q and 320 use R. 100 use both P and Q, 120 use both Q and R, 190 use exactly two of the apps and 40 use all three.

How many people use none of the three apps?

- **A.** 140  _(error: ignored the all-three correction)_
- **B.** 220 ✅
- **C.** 180  _(error: subtracted the all-three group only once)_
- **D.** 260  _(error: added the all-three group back)_

**Working**

1. n(at least one) = Σn − (exactly two) − 2 × (all three)
2. = 1,050 − 190 − 80 = 780
3. None = 1,000 − 780 = 220

**Formula:** n(∪) = Σn − E₂ − 2E₃  
**Trap:** Each all-three user is counted three times in Σn.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-383 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

A survey of 1,000 people asked which of three apps P, Q and R they use. Results: 370 use P, 360 use Q and 320 use R. 100 use both P and Q, 120 use both Q and R, 190 use exactly two of the apps and 40 use all three.

How many people use exactly one app?

- **A.** 550 ✅
- **B.** 590  _(error: included the all-three users)_
- **C.** 780  _(error: gave at least one)_
- **D.** 510  _(error: subtracted all-three once more)_

**Working**

1. Only Q = 360 − 60 − 80 − 40 = 180
2. Only R = 320 − 80 − 50 − 40 = 150
3. Exactly one = 220 + 180 + 150 = 550

**Formula:** Exactly one = at least one − E₂ − E₃  
**Trap:** Work region by region.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-384 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

A survey of 1,000 people asked which of three apps P, Q and R they use. Results: 370 use P, 360 use Q and 320 use R. 100 use both P and Q, 120 use both Q and R, 190 use exactly two of the apps and 40 use all three.

What percentage of R users also use at least one other app?

- **A.** 21.79%  _(error: divided by app users)_
- **B.** 53.13% ✅
- **C.** 17%  _(error: divided by all surveyed)_
- **D.** 40.63%  _(error: left out the all-three users)_

**Working**

1. R users with another app = 80 + 50 + 40 = 170
2. 170/320 × 100 = 53.13%

**Formula:** Conditional share  
**Trap:** The base is R users.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-385 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

A survey of 1,000 people asked which of three apps P, Q and R they use. Results: 370 use P, 360 use Q and 320 use R. 100 use both P and Q, 120 use both Q and R, 190 use exactly two of the apps and 40 use all three.

If 30 of the people who use only Q start using R as well, how many people will use exactly two apps?

- **A.** 190  _(error: did not update the regions)_
- **B.** 250  _(error: added 30 to two regions)_
- **C.** 220 ✅
- **D.** 160  _(error: subtracted 30 from exactly two)_

**Working**

1. Only Q → Q and R only: 80 + 30 = 110
2. Exactly two = 60 + 110 + 50 = 220

**Formula:** Move people between regions  
**Trap:** They move from 'only Q' into 'Q and R only'.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-386 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

Each of 720 students plays at least one of cricket, football and hockey. The number who play exactly one game is twice the number who play exactly two games. 60 students play all three. Those who play only cricket, only football and only hockey are in the ratio 5 : 4 : 2. 90 students play cricket and football but not hockey, and 70 play football and hockey but not cricket.

How many students play cricket?

- **A.** 350  _(error: left out the all-three group)_
- **B.** 290  _(error: left out the cricket-hockey-only and all-three groups)_
- **C.** 470  _(error: counted the all-three group twice)_
- **D.** 410 ✅

**Working**

1. Exactly one + exactly two + 60 = 720 and exactly one = 2 × exactly two ⇒ exactly two = 220, exactly one = 440
2. Only C = 440 × 5/11 = 200; C and H only = 220 − 90 − 70 = 60
3. Cricket = 200 + 90 + 60 + 60 = 410

**Formula:** Region method  
**Trap:** Include every region inside the cricket circle.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-387 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

Each of 720 students plays at least one of cricket, football and hockey. The number who play exactly one game is twice the number who play exactly two games. 60 students play all three. Those who play only cricket, only football and only hockey are in the ratio 5 : 4 : 2. 90 students play cricket and football but not hockey, and 70 play football and hockey but not cricket.

How many students play exactly two games?

- **A.** 440  _(error: gave exactly one)_
- **B.** 240  _(error: ignored the all-three group when splitting 720)_
- **C.** 220 ✅
- **D.** 280  _(error: included the all-three group)_

**Working**

1. 3 × (exactly two) + 60 = 720 ⇒ exactly two = 220

**Formula:** E₁ + E₂ + E₃ = total  
**Trap:** Remove the all-three group first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-388 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

Each of 720 students plays at least one of cricket, football and hockey. The number who play exactly one game is twice the number who play exactly two games. 60 students play all three. Those who play only cricket, only football and only hockey are in the ratio 5 : 4 : 2. 90 students play cricket and football but not hockey, and 70 play football and hockey but not cricket.

How many students play football but not cricket?

- **A.** 230 ✅
- **B.** 160  _(error: gave only football)_
- **C.** 180  _(error: subtracted only-cricket from football)_
- **D.** 290  _(error: included the all-three group)_

**Working**

1. Only F = 440 × 4/11 = 160
2. F and H only = 70
3. Football but not cricket = 230

**Formula:** F − (F ∩ C)  
**Trap:** All-three players play cricket.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-389 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

Each of 720 students plays at least one of cricket, football and hockey. The number who play exactly one game is twice the number who play exactly two games. 60 students play all three. Those who play only cricket, only football and only hockey are in the ratio 5 : 4 : 2. 90 students play cricket and football but not hockey, and 70 play football and hockey but not cricket.

What is the ratio of hockey players to football players?

- **A.** 21 : 32  _(error: excluded the all-three group)_
- **B.** 38 : 27  _(error: reversed the ratio)_
- **C.** 27 : 38 ✅
- **D.** 1 : 2  _(error: compared only-hockey with only-football)_

**Working**

1. Hockey = 80 + 70 + 60 + 60 = 270
2. Football = 160 + 90 + 70 + 60 = 380
3. 270 : 380 = 27 : 38

**Formula:** Ratio of set totals  
**Trap:** Build totals from all regions.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-390 · L4 · hard · Set-based data interpretation · officer

Each of 720 students plays at least one of cricket, football and hockey. The number who play exactly one game is twice the number who play exactly two games. 60 students play all three. Those who play only cricket, only football and only hockey are in the ratio 5 : 4 : 2. 90 students play cricket and football but not hockey, and 70 play football and hockey but not cricket.

What percentage of the students play at most one game?

- **A.** 30.56%  _(error: used exactly two)_
- **B.** 69.44%  _(error: added the all-three group)_
- **C.** 61.11% ✅
- **D.** 91.67%  _(error: computed at most two)_

**Working**

1. Everyone plays at least one ⇒ at most one = exactly one = 440
2. 440/720 × 100 = 61.11%

**Formula:** Share  
**Trap:** 'At most one' here equals 'exactly one'.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-391 · L2 · medium · Mixed and combined DI · foundation

A college has 1,500 students. A pie chart gives the share of each stream, and the table beside it gives the ratio of boys to girls in each stream.

| Stream | Share of students | Boys : Girls |
|---|---|---|
| Science | 30% | 3 : 2 |
| Commerce | 24% | 5 : 3 |
| Arts | 20% | 2 : 3 |
| Engineering | 16% | 7 : 5 |
| Law | 10% | 1 : 1 |

How many girls are there in Arts?

- **A.** 60  _(error: gave the difference)_
- **B.** 150  _(error: assumed an equal split)_
- **C.** 180 ✅
- **D.** 120  _(error: computed boys)_

**Working**

1. Arts = 20% of 1,500 = 300
2. Girls = 300 × 3/5 = 180

**Formula:** Part = total × share of ratio  
**Trap:** Boys : girls = 2 : 3 ⇒ girls are 3/5.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-392 · L2 · medium · Mixed and combined DI · foundation

A college has 1,500 students. A pie chart gives the share of each stream, and the table beside it gives the ratio of boys to girls in each stream.

|---|---|---|

| Engineering | 16% | 7 : 5 |

- **C.** 670  _(error: computed girls)_

**Working**

**Formula:** Σ stream-wise boys

## QAB-393 · L2 · medium · Mixed and combined DI · foundation

A college has 1,500 students. A pie chart gives the share of each stream, and the table beside it gives the ratio of boys to girls in each stream.

| Stream | Share of students | Boys : Girls |
|---|---|---|
| Science | 30% | 3 : 2 |

What is the ratio of girls in Science to girls in Commerce?

**Working**

3. 180 : 135 = 4 : 3

**Formula:** Ratio of derived counts

## QAB-394 · L2 · medium · Mixed and combined DI · foundation

A college has 1,500 students. A pie chart gives the share of each stream, and the table beside it gives the ratio of boys to girls in each stream.

| Stream | Share of students | Boys : Girls |
|---|---|---|
| Science | 30% | 3 : 2 |

| Arts | 20% | 2 : 3 |
| Engineering | 16% | 7 : 5 |

## QAB-395 · L2 · medium · Mixed and combined DI · foundation

A college has 1,500 students. A pie chart gives the share of each stream, and the table beside it gives the ratio of boys to girls in each stream.

| Stream | Share of students | Boys : Girls |
|---|---|---|

## QAB-396 · L4 · hard · Mixed and combined DI · officer

Table 1 gives the units manufactured (in thousands) by four companies in a year and the percentage of manufactured units sold. Table 2 gives the selling price per unit.

| Company | Manufactured ('000) | Sold (%) |
|---|---|---|
| A | 250 | 80% |
| B | 320 | 75% |
| C | 180 | 90% |
| D | 400 | 70% |

| Company | Price per unit (₹) |
|---|---|
| A | 1,200 |
| B | 1,000 |
| C | 1,500 |
| D | 900 |

How many manufactured units remained unsold across the four companies?

- **A.** 2,44,375  _(error: applied the average sale percentage to the total)_
- **B.** 1,48,000  _(error: left out D)_
- **C.** 8,82,000  _(error: gave the units sold)_
- **D.** 2,68,000 ✅

**Working**

1. Unsold: A 50, B 80, C 18, D 120 thousand
2. Total = 2,68,000

**Formula:** Unsold = manufactured × (100 − sold %)  
**Trap:** Rates differ across companies.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-397 · L4 · hard · Mixed and combined DI · officer

Table 1 gives the units manufactured (in thousands) by four companies in a year and the percentage of manufactured units sold. Table 2 gives the selling price per unit.

| Company | Manufactured ('000) | Sold (%) |
|---|---|---|
| A | 250 | 80% |
| B | 320 | 75% |
| C | 180 | 90% |
| D | 400 | 70% |

| Company | Price per unit (₹) |
|---|---|
| A | 1,200 |
| B | 1,000 |
| C | 1,500 |
| D | 900 |

Which company earned the highest revenue from sales?

- **A.** Company D ✅
- **B.** Company B  _(error: picked the highest units sold after D without computing revenue)_
- **C.** Company A  _(error: picked the highest sale percentage after C)_
- **D.** Company C  _(error: picked the highest price)_

**Working**

1. Revenue: A ₹24 crore, B ₹24 crore, C ₹24.3 crore, D ₹25.2 crore
2. Highest: D

**Formula:** Revenue = units sold × price

## QAB-398 · L4 · hard · Mixed and combined DI · officer

Table 1 gives the units manufactured (in thousands) by four companies in a year and the percentage of manufactured units sold. Table 2 gives the selling price per unit.

## QAB-399 · L4 · hard · Mixed and combined DI · officer

Table 1 gives the units manufactured (in thousands) by four companies in a year and the percentage of manufactured units sold. Table 2 gives the selling price per unit.

| Company | Manufactured ('000) | Sold (%) |
|---|---|---|
| A | 250 | 80% |
| B | 320 | 75% |
| C | 180 | 90% |
| D | 400 | 70% |

| Company | Price per unit (₹) |
|---|---|
| A | 1,200 |
| B | 1,000 |
| C | 1,500 |
| D | 900 |

Had D sold 80% of its units at the same price, how much more revenue would it have earned?

- **A.** ₹2.52 crore  _(error: took 10% of the current revenue)_
- **B.** ₹3.60 crore ✅
- **C.** ₹0.36 crore  _(error: treated the data as hundreds, not thousands)_
- **D.** ₹4.80 crore  _(error: used A's price)_

**Working**

1. Extra units = 10% of 4,00,000 = 40,000
2. 40,000 × 900 = ₹3.6 crore

**Formula:** Extra revenue = extra units × price  
**Trap:** 10 percentage points of manufactured units.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-400 · L4 · hard · Mixed and combined DI · officer

Table 1 gives the units manufactured (in thousands) by four companies in a year and the percentage of manufactured units sold. Table 2 gives the selling price per unit.

| Company | Manufactured ('000) | Sold (%) |
|---|---|---|
| A | 250 | 80% |
| B | 320 | 75% |
| C | 180 | 90% |
| D | 400 | 70% |

| Company | Price per unit (₹) |
|---|---|
| A | 1,200 |
| B | 1,000 |
| C | 1,500 |
| D | 900 |

What is the average price realised per unit sold, taking all four companies together?

- **A.** ₹1,105.44 ✅
- **B.** ₹1,150  _(error: took the simple average of prices)_
- **C.** ₹1,100  _(error: took the median price)_
- **D.** ₹1,086.96  _(error: weighted prices by units manufactured)_

**Working**

1. Total revenue = ₹97.5 crore
2. Units sold = 8,82,000
3. Average = 97,50,00,000/8,82,000 = ₹1,105.44

**Formula:** Weighted average = total revenue / total units sold  
**Trap:** Weights are units sold.

| Thursday | 15% | 20% |
| Friday | 20% | 15% |
| Saturday | 20% | 30% |
| Sunday | 15% | 25% |

How many foreign tourists visited during the week?

- **B.** 506  _(error: averaged the daily foreign percentages)_
- **C.** 1,878  _(error: counted domestic tourists)_
- **D.** 378  _(error: left out Saturday)_

1. Foreign: 48, 72, 24, 72, 72, 144, 90
2. Total = 522

**Formula:** Σ day-wise values  
**Trap:** Daily totals differ, so percentages cannot be averaged.

| Friday | 20% | 15% |
| Saturday | 20% | 30% |
| Sunday | 15% | 25% |

- **B.** 4 : 3  _(error: compared all tourists)_

3. 408 : 270 = 68 : 45

**Formula:** Ratio of derived counts

## QAB-406 · L1 · easy · Data sufficiency · foundation

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the value of the positive integer x?

I. x² = 49
II. x + 3 = 10

- **A.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **B.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **C.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_
- **D.** Either I or II alone is sufficient ✅

**Working**

1. I: x² = 49 and x > 0 ⇒ x = 7 (sufficient)
2. II: x = 7 (sufficient)
3. Either statement alone answers it.
4. Answer: Either I or II alone is sufficient.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Positivity rules out −7 in statement I.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-407 · L2 · medium · Data sufficiency · foundation

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is A's present age?

I. A is 5 years older than B.
II. B is 20 years old.

- **A.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **B.** Both I and II together are needed ✅
- **C.** Even I and II together are insufficient  _(error: missed that the statements combine to a unique answer)_
- **D.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_

**Working**

1. I relates A and B but gives no number
2. II gives B only
3. Together: A = 25
4. Answer: Both I and II together are needed.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Neither statement alone fixes A.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-408 · L2 · medium · Data sufficiency · foundation

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

Is the integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100) even?

I. n is divisible by 6.
II. n is divisible by 3.

- **A.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **B.** Either I or II alone is sufficient  _(error: treated a partially informative statement as sufficient on its own)_
- **C.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_
- **D.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not ✅

**Working**

1. I: every multiple of 6 is even ⇒ Yes (sufficient)
2. II: 3 is odd, 6 is even ⇒ not sufficient
3. Answer: I alone is sufficient; II alone is not.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** A definite 'Yes' is sufficient, just as a number would be.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-409 · L2 · medium · Data sufficiency · foundation

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the price of one pen (in whole rupees)?

I. 3 pens and 2 pencils cost ₹40.
II. One pencil costs ₹5.

- **A.** Both I and II together are needed ✅
- **B.** Even I and II together are insufficient  _(error: missed that the statements combine to a unique answer)_
- **C.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **D.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_

**Working**

1. I: 3p + 2c = 40 has several solutions (p = 10, c = 5; p = 12, c = 2; …)
2. II gives only the pencil
3. Together: 3p = 30 ⇒ p = ₹10
4. Answer: Both I and II together are needed.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** One equation in two unknowns is not enough.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-410 · L2 · medium · Data sufficiency · foundation

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the two-digit number?

I. The sum of its digits is 9.
II. Its digits differ by 3.

- **A.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **B.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **C.** Even I and II together are insufficient ✅
- **D.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_

**Working**

1. I: 18, 27, 36, 45, …
2. II: 14, 25, 36, 41, 63, …
3. Together: 36 or 63 ⇒ still two answers
4. Answer: Even I and II together are insufficient.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Digits differing by 3 does not say which digit is larger.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-411 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is B's present age?

I. The ratio of the present ages of A and B is 4 : 5.
II. Six years ago the ratio of their ages was 3 : 4.

- **A.** Even I and II together are insufficient  _(error: missed that the statements combine to a unique answer)_
- **B.** Both I and II together are needed ✅
- **C.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **D.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_

**Working**

1. I: A = 4k, B = 5k (k unknown)
2. II: one equation in two unknowns
3. Together: (4k − 6)/(5k − 6) = 3/4 ⇒ k = 6 ⇒ B = 30
4. Answer: Both I and II together are needed.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Two ratios at two points in time are needed.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-412 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the two-digit number N?

I. N is a multiple of 7 and its digit sum is 10.
II. The number formed by reversing N's digits exceeds N by 54.

- **A.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **B.** Both I and II together are needed ✅
- **C.** Even I and II together are insufficient  _(error: missed that the statements combine to a unique answer)_
- **D.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_

**Working**

1. I: 28 or 91
2. II: units − tens = 6 ⇒ 17, 28, 39
3. Together: 28
4. Answer: Both I and II together are needed.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Check that each statement alone leaves more than one number.

## QAB-413 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

Is x > y? (x, y are integers.)

I. x + y = 10
II. x − y = 4

- **A.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **B.** Either I or II alone is sufficient  _(error: treated a partially informative statement as sufficient on its own)_
- **C.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_
- **D.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not ✅

**Working**

1. I: (7, 3) gives Yes, (3, 7) gives No ⇒ not sufficient
2. II: x − y = 4 > 0 ⇒ Yes (sufficient)
3. Answer: II alone is sufficient; I alone is not.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Solving for x and y is unnecessary; II already decides the comparison.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-414 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the remainder when the positive integer N is divided by 12?

I. N leaves remainder 1 when divided by 4.
II. N leaves remainder 2 when divided by 3.

- **A.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **B.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **C.** Even I and II together are insufficient  _(error: missed that the statements combine to a unique answer)_
- **D.** Both I and II together are needed ✅

**Working**

1. I: N mod 12 ∈ {1, 5, 9}
2. II: N mod 12 ∈ {2, 5, 8, 11}
3. Together: N mod 12 = 5
4. Answer: Both I and II together are needed.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Neither remainder alone fixes N mod 12; combined (CRT) they do.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-415 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the remainder when the positive integer N is divided by 6?

I. N leaves remainder 7 when divided by 12.
II. N leaves remainder 1 when divided by 3.

- **A.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **B.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not ✅
- **C.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_
- **D.** Either I or II alone is sufficient  _(error: treated a partially informative statement as sufficient on its own)_

**Working**

1. I: N = 12k + 7 ⇒ N mod 6 = 1 (sufficient)
2. II: N mod 6 is 1 or 4 ⇒ not sufficient
3. Answer: I alone is sufficient; II alone is not.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Because 6 divides 12, statement I alone fixes the remainder.

## QAB-416 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

A and B together finish a job in 12 days. In how many days can A alone finish it?

I. B alone can finish it in 20 days.
II. A is 50% more efficient than B.

- **A.** Either I or II alone is sufficient ✅
- **B.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **C.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **D.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_

**Working**

1. I: 1/A = 1/12 − 1/20 = 1/30 ⇒ 30 days
2. II: rates 3 : 2 ⇒ A's rate = (3/5)(1/12) = 1/20 ⇒ 20 days
3. Each alone suffices.
4. Answer: Either I or II alone is sufficient.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** The combined-time fact is given in the question; each statement completes it.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAB-417 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the simple interest earned in 3 years?

I. The principal is ₹8,000 and the rate is 10% per annum.
II. The amount after 3 years is ₹10,400.

- **A.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not ✅
- **B.** Either I or II alone is sufficient  _(error: treated a partially informative statement as sufficient on its own)_
- **C.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **D.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_

**Working**

1. I: SI = 8,000 × 10% × 3 = ₹2,400 (sufficient)
2. II: P(1 + 3r/100) = 10,400 has many (P, r) pairs, e.g. 8,000 & 10%, 6,500 & 20%
3. Answer: I alone is sufficient; II alone is not.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** The amount alone does not separate principal from interest.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-418 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the integer x?

I. x² − 5x + 6 = 0
II. x is a prime number.

- **A.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **B.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **C.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_
- **D.** Even I and II together are insufficient ✅

**Working**

1. I: x = 2 or 3
2. II: any prime
3. Together: 2 and 3 are both prime ⇒ still two values
4. Answer: Even I and II together are insufficient.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** Test whether the extra condition actually removes a root.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-419 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the average of five consecutive odd integers?

I. The largest of them is 19.
II. The sum of the smallest and the largest is 30.

- **A.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_
- **B.** Both I and II together are needed  _(error: missed that one statement already fixes the answer)_
- **C.** Either I or II alone is sufficient ✅
- **D.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_

**Working**

1. I: numbers 11 to 19 ⇒ average 15
2. II: average = (smallest + largest)/2 = 15
3. Each alone suffices.
4. Answer: Either I or II alone is sufficient.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** For equally spaced numbers, average = (first + last)/2.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-420 · L3 · hard · Data sufficiency · officer

Each question has two statements, I and II. Decide whether the data in the statements are sufficient to answer it.

What is the ratio of the speeds of A and B (in whole km/h)?

I. A covers 180 km in 3 hours.
II. B takes 1 hour more than A to cover 180 km.

- **A.** I alone is sufficient; II alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement II adds / over-read statement I)_
- **B.** Even I and II together are insufficient  _(error: missed that the statements combine to a unique answer)_
- **C.** Both I and II together are needed ✅
- **D.** II alone is sufficient; I alone is not  _(error: overlooked what statement I adds / over-read statement II)_

**Working**

1. I: A = 60 km/h, B unknown
2. II: (60, 45) and (90, 60) both fit ⇒ ratio 4 : 3 or 3 : 2
3. Together: B = 180/4 = 45 ⇒ 4 : 3
4. Answer: Both I and II together are needed.

**Formula:** Data sufficiency: test each statement alone, then together  
**Trap:** A time difference alone does not fix a ratio.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-421 · L1 · easy · Area and perimeter — rectangle, square, triangle · foundation

The perimeter of a rectangle measuring 18 m by 12 m is:

- **A.** 30 m  _(error: did not double (semi-perimeter))_
- **B.** 48 m  _(error: doubled only the length)_
- **C.** 216 m  _(error: computed the area)_
- **D.** 60 m ✅

**Working**

1. P = 2(l + b) = 2(18 + 12) = 60 m

**Formula:** Area/perimeter formulas for rectangle, square and triangle  
**Trap:** Match the formula to the figure and keep units consistent.

## QAB-425 · L2 · medium · Area and perimeter — rectangle, square, triangle · foundation

The length of a rectangle is 20% more than its breadth and its perimeter is 88 cm. Its area is:

- **A.** 480 cm² ✅
- **B.** 484 cm²  _(error: assumed a square of the same perimeter)_
- **C.** 1,920 cm²  _(error: took 88 as the semi-perimeter)_
- **D.** 400 cm²  _(error: took the breadth as both sides)_

**Working**

1. 2(1.2b + b) = 88 ⇒ 4.4b = 88 ⇒ b = 20, l = 24
2. Area = 24 × 20 = 480 cm²

**Formula:** Area/perimeter formulas for rectangle, square and triangle  
**Trap:** Match the formula to the figure and keep units consistent.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAB-426 · L2 · medium · Area and perimeter — rectangle, square, triangle · officer

The legs of a right triangle are in the ratio 3 : 4 and its hypotenuse is 25 cm. Its area is:

**Working**

1. Legs 3k, 4k; 5k = 25 ⇒ k = 5 ⇒ legs 15, 20
2. Area = ½ × 15 × 20 = 150 cm²

**Formula:** Area/perimeter formulas for rectangle, square and triangle

## QAB-427 · L3 · hard · Area and perimeter — rectangle, square, triangle · officer

The length of a rectangle is increased by 30% and its breadth is decreased by 20%. The area:

- **C.** increases by 16%  _(error: added the product term instead of subtracting it)_
- **D.** increases by 10%  _(error: added the percentages (30 − 20))_

**Working**

1. New area factor = 1.3 × 0.8 = 1.04
2. Net change = 30 − 20 − (30 × 20)/100 = +4%

**Formula:** Net % = a + b + ab/100 (with signs)  
**Trap:** Successive percentage changes multiply.

## QAB-428 · L3 · hard · Area and perimeter — rectangle, square, triangle · officer

A path 2.5 m wide runs around the outside of a 40 m × 30 m garden. At ₹12 per m², the cost of paving the path is:

- **A.** ₹4,500 ✅

- **C.** ₹3,900  _(error: treated the path as inside the garden)_
- **D.** ₹2,175  _(error: added the width only once to each dimension)_

**Working**

1. Outer rectangle = 45 × 35 = 1,575 m²
2. Path = 1,575 − 1,200 = 375 m²
3. Cost = 375 × 12 = ₹4,500

**Formula:** Path area = outer area − inner area  
**Trap:** A path on both sides adds twice the width to each dimension.

A triangle has sides 13 cm, 14 cm and 15 cm. The altitude on the 14 cm side is:

**Working**

1. s = 21; Area = √(21 × 8 × 7 × 6) = 84 cm²
2. Altitude = 2 × 84/14 = 12 cm

**Formula:** Heron: √(s(s−a)(s−b)(s−c)); h = 2A/base

## QAB-443 · L3 · hard · Circle — area, circumference and sectors · officer

The outer and inner circumferences of a circular ring are 176 cm and 132 cm. The area of the ring is (π = 22/7):

**Working**

1. R = 176/(2π) = 28, r = 132/(2π) = 21

Two circles of radii 8 cm and 3 cm have centres 13 cm apart. The length of their direct common tangent is:

**Working**

1. Direct tangent = √(d² − (R − r)²) = √(169 − 25) = 12 cm

## QAB-460 · L3 · hard · Circle theorems — chords, tangents and cyclic quadrilaterals · officer

In a cyclic quadrilateral ABCD, ∠BAD = (2x + 10)° and ∠BCD = (3x − 5)°. Then ∠BCD is:

- **D.** 35°  _(error: gave x)_

**Working**

1. 2x + 10 + 3x − 5 = 180 ⇒ x = 35
2. ∠BCD = 100°

**Formula:** Opposite angles of a cyclic quadrilateral sum to 180°  
**Trap:** Opposite angles are supplementary, not equal.

## QAB-461 · L3 · hard · Circle theorems — chords, tangents and cyclic quadrilaterals · officer

Two parallel chords of lengths 24 cm and 10 cm lie on the same side of the centre of a circle of radius 13 cm. The distance between them is:

- **A.** 14 cm  _(error: subtracted the chord lengths)_
- **B.** 12 cm  _(error: gave the distance of one chord)_
- **C.** 7 cm ✅
- **D.** 17 cm  _(error: placed them on opposite sides)_

**Working**

1. Distances from centre: √(169 − 144) = 5 and √(169 − 25) = 12
2. Same side ⇒ 12 − 5 = 7 cm

**Formula:** d = √(r² − (chord/2)²)  
**Trap:** Same side subtracts, opposite sides add.

## QAB-462 · L3 · hard · Circle theorems — chords, tangents and cyclic quadrilaterals · officer

- **C.** 122°  _(error: subtracted 58° from 180°)_
- **D.** 116° ✅

**Working**

1. Angle in alternate segment = 58°
2. ∠AOB = 2 × 58° = 116°

**Formula:** Tangent–chord angle = inscribed angle on the other side

## QAB-497 · L1 · easy · Lines and angles · foundation

1. Overhang each side = (18 − 12)/2 = 3 ⇒ height = √(25 − 9) = 4

## QAB-532 · L3 · hard · Reshaping and equal-perimeter problems · officer

## QAB-535 · L3 · hard · Reshaping and equal-perimeter problems · officer

A rope encloses a square of side 22 m. If the same rope is laid as a circle, how much more area does it enclose? (π = 22/7)

- **A.** 0 m²  _(error: assumed the area is unchanged)_
- **B.** 330 m²  _(error: used r = 7 and took the difference)_
- **C.** 132 m² ✅
- **D.** 616 m²  _(error: gave the circle's area, not the gain)_

**Working**

1. Rope = 88 m ⇒ r = 14 ⇒ circle area 616 m²
2. Gain = 616 − 484 = 132 m²

**Formula:** Perimeter conserved  
**Trap:** Circle maximises area for a given perimeter.

## QAB-536 · L3 · hard · Reshaping and equal-perimeter problems · officer

A solid cone of base radius 12 cm and height 24 cm is melted into spheres of radius 2 cm. The number of spheres is:

- **A.** 36  _(error: took the sphere volume as 32π (dropped the ÷ 3))_
- **B.** 324  _(error: forgot the ⅓ for the cone)_
- **C.** 72  _(error: used the sphere's surface area)_
- **D.** 108 ✅

**Working**

1. Cone = ⅓π × 144 × 24 = 1,152π
2. Sphere = (4/3)π × 8 = 32π/3
3. Number = 1,152 × 3/32 = 108

**Formula:** n = V(cone)/V(sphere)  
**Trap:** Keep both fractions.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

**Working**

1. Wire = 44 + 28 = 72 cm
2. Side = 18 ⇒ area = 324 cm²

**Formula:** Semicircle perimeter = πr + 2r  
**Trap:** Include the straight edge.

2. V = 22/7 × 49 × 18 = 2,772 cm³

## QAB-546 · L3 · hard · Triangle properties and centres · officer

In ΔABC, AB = 10 cm, AC = 14 cm and BC = 12 cm. The length of the median from A is:

## QAB-564 · L3 · hard · Volume and surface area of solids · officer

1. Tank volume = 50 × 44 × 0.07 = 154 m³
